# Canon Ledger - Dungeon Crawler Carl
Owner: DCC Lore | Status: review | Version: 1.20 | Updated: 2026-10-06
Summary: Master "hard memory" of DCC canon findings, each with a source quote (<=25 words), source tag and VERIFIED / WIKI-ONLY / UNVERIFIED confidence. Spoilers through Book 8.

## 0. How to read this ledger (rules in brief; full procedure in `ledger-rules.md`)
- **Nothing here was checked against the novels' text.** Every source is a fan wiki mirror, a fan site, or a YouTube recap/transcript kept in this folder (`raw/pages/`, `sources/`). "VERIFIED" is therefore a *bookkeeping label with a defined meaning*, not proof from the book.
- **Confidence labels**
  - **VERIFIED** = (a) the wiki page/row carries a book-chapter citation (shown as `cite B# ch#`; `page cite` = a chapter cite exists elsewhere on the same page but not on the quoted sentence; `chapter-summary row` = the row comes from the wiki's per-chapter summary table), **or** (b) two independent source families agree (shown as `2 sources`; families: Fandom wiki, dungeon-crawler-carl.wiki `FW2`, dungeoncrawlercarl.nl `NL`, YouTube recap `YT`). The Fandom wiki's master `Achievement` page and its per-achievement pages are the *same* family and never count as two sources.
  - **WIKI-ONLY** = one wiki source, no chapter citation.
  - **UNVERIFIED** = no usable quote, incomplete wiki infobox, unresolved conflict, single fan-site claim, or a claim the sources themselves hedge.
- **Quotes** are exact substrings of the cited local file (cites and whitespace normalised; a leading/trailing `…` marks trimming; max 25 words). Wiki prose is a *wiki* quote, not novel text; achievement/AI text is the wiki's transcription of System text. `tools/ledger_check.py` re-verifies every quote mechanically.
- **Source tags**: `W:<page>` = `raw/pages/<page>.txt` (Fandom wiki mirror); `FW2:<file>` = `sources/fanwiki/`; `NL:<file>` = `sources/nl/`; `YT:<id>` = `sources/youtube/<id>.txt` (auto-captions; garbled names). Reddit was blocked (403) and is only referenced in the Conflicts section as unread snippets.
- **IDs** are permanent (A-, M-, L-, S-/P-/I-/C-/R-, B-, H-, T-, X-). **Date** column = date the entry was added. Never renumber; append (see `ledger-rules.md`).
- Chapter cite style `B1 ch5` = Book 1, chapter 5 as cited by the wiki. Floor ordinals: F3 = Third Floor. Book to floors: B1 F1-2, B2 F3, B3 F4, B4 F5, B5 F6-7, B6 F8, B7 F9, B8 F10-11.

## 2. Mechanics rulings

| ID | Ruling (paraphrase) | Source quote (<=25 words) | Source | Confidence | Date |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| M-001 | The first-kill achievement is required to begin earning experience. | "This achievement is required to begin earning experience points in the Dungeon." | W:You've Killed a Mob! Achievement; NL:achievements-and-loot-boxes-reward-system | VERIFIED (cite B1 ch5) | 2026-10-03 |
| M-002 | Each level-up grants 3 stat points, undistributable until race/class selection on Floor 3. | "Crawlers typically gain (3) stat points each time they level up, but cannot distribute the stat points until they choose a race and class on …" | W:Level-Up, Baby! Achievement | VERIFIED (cite B1 ch4) | 2026-10-03 |
| M-003 | A sixth stat, Wisdom, exists but crawlers cannot view or adjust it. | "A sixth stat, Wisdom, also exists, but crawlers cannot view or directly adjust it — the showrunners discovered that changing a person's Wisdom dramatically rewired …" | W:Player Stats | VERIFIED (cite B1 ch4) | 2026-10-03 |
| M-004 | A new adult human starts with roughly 3-5 in each core stat; 6 is solidly above average. | "… human arrives with somewhere between three and five points in each of the five core stats, and a six counts as a solidly above-average score." | W:Player Stats | VERIFIED (cite B1 ch4) | 2026-10-03 |
| M-005 | INT sets max mana, regen rate, and ability to recall information/comprehend spellbooks. | "Intelligence determines a crawler's maximum mana points (MP), their rate of mana regeneration, and their ability to recall information and comprehend spellbooks." | W:Player Stats | VERIFIED (cite B1 ch29) | 2026-10-03 |
| M-006 | CON governs the potion cooldown window (Carl ~15 s, Donut ~2 min). | "The higher the Constitution, the shorter that window: Carl's cooldown ran about fifteen seconds, while the low-Constitution Donut had to wait closer to two minutes." | W:Player Stats; NL:what-does-constitution-do | VERIFIED (cite B1 ch24) | 2026-10-03 |
| M-007 | At base 100 in a stat: achievement and three minutes to choose a permanent upgrade. | "When a crawler reaches a base score of 100 in any stat, they earn an achievement and are given three minutes to choose one of …" | W:Player Stats | VERIFIED (page cite B1 ch29) | 2026-10-03 |
| M-008 | Skills generally cap at level 15. | "In general, skills cap at level 15." | W:Skills | VERIFIED (page cite B2 ch2) | 2026-10-03 |
| M-009 | Cockroach (Donut): chance to survive a free lethal hit once per fight. | "Cockroach is a skill that gives you a chance to survive a free lethal hit once a fight." | W:Cockroach Skill | VERIFIED (page cite B2 ch2) | 2026-10-03 |
| M-010 | Safe rooms enforce a three-strike rule against violence/theft. | "It is "a strict, ironclad rule" with a three strikes policy:" | W:Safe Rooms | VERIFIED (page cite B1 ch5) | 2026-10-03 |
| M-011 | Loot boxes can only be opened (and stat points distributed) inside a Safe Room. | "Crawlers must be inside a Safe Room to open Loot Boxes, or, after Third Floor race and class selection, to distribute any stat points earned …" | W:Safe Rooms | VERIFIED (cite B1 ch5) | 2026-10-03 |
| M-012 | Safe rooms close one hour before level collapse (from Floor 2), ejecting occupants. | "Starting on the Second Floor, all safe rooms close one hour before level collapse, teleporting any crawlers still inside to just outside the room." | W:Safe Rooms | VERIFIED (cite B1 ch39) | 2026-10-03 |
| M-013 | A mob that attacks someone inside a safe room is teleported away (~1 square mile). | "A mob will be teleported to a random place within one square mile (2.59 km2) of the safe room if it attempts to attack anyone …" | W:Safe Rooms | VERIFIED (cite B1 ch5) | 2026-10-03 |
| M-014 | Six main boss tiers: Neighborhood, Borough, City, Province, Country, Floor. | "There are six main types of standard bosses, scaling up in difficulty and jurisdiction: Neighborhood, Borough, City, Province, Country, and Floor bosses." | W:Boss; NL:from-neighborhood-boss-to-dungeon-boss | VERIFIED (cite B1 ch12) | 2026-10-03 |
| M-015 | Boss Box tier and loot quality scale with the boss defeated. | "The box's tier, and the quality of the loot inside, scales with the difficulty of the boss defeated." | W:Boss Box | VERIFIED (cite B1 ch12) | 2026-10-03 |
| M-016 | The number of stairwells halves with each floor. | "The number of stairwells halves with each descent: there were 150,000 entrances from Earth down to the First Floor, 75,000 down to the Second Floor, …" | W:Stairwells | VERIFIED (cite B1 ch1) | 2026-10-03 |
| M-017 | Each crawler is limited to three patrons (sponsors). | "Every crawler is limited to three patrons; all three spots are auctioned for one day upon the induction of the fourth, fifth, and sixth floors; …" | W:Sponsors | VERIFIED (page cite B1 ch4) | 2026-10-03 |
| M-018 | Party Leader is the member with the highest combined base stat total. | "The party member with the highest combined base stat total is designated the Party Leader." | W:Party | VERIFIED (page cite B1 ch4) | 2026-10-03 |
| M-019 | Personal spaces are sold by Bopca proprietors in safe rooms. | "Personal spaces are sold by Bopca proprietors in the safe rooms." | W:Personal Space | VERIFIED (page cite B3 ch2) | 2026-10-03 |
| M-020 | Most quests are optional and lapse when the crawler leaves the issuing floor. | "Most quests are optional and bound to the floor that issued them; an unfinished quest lapses when the crawler leaves the level." | W:Quest | VERIFIED (cite B2 ch15) | 2026-10-03 |
| M-021 | Hunters are outworlders who arrive on the Sixth Floor to hunt Crawlers for gear. | "… into the Dungeon on the Sixth Floor to hunt Crawlers for their gear to sell to the Faction leaders for Faction Wars on the Ninth …" | W:Hunters | WIKI-ONLY | 2026-10-03 |

| M-023 | The Prize Carousel has nine curtained sections holding nine prizes. | "It has nine curtained-off sections containing nine prizes." | W:Prize Carousel | VERIFIED (page cite B3 ch7) | 2026-10-03 |
| M-024 | Crafting tables level up each time a floor collapses. | "Tables level up automatically each time a floor collapses, so tables acquired early grow stronger than those acquired later." | W:Crafting | VERIFIED (cite B3 ch2) | 2026-10-03 |
| M-025 | Ring of Divine Suffering: a marked target blocks the wearer from healing until it dies. | "Until the mark dies, the wearer cannot heal, and any injury, poison, or other ill effect persists." | W:Enchanted Night Wyrm's Ring of Divine Suffering | VERIFIED (cite B3 ch19) | 2026-10-03 |
| M-026 | The Gate of the Feral Gods bridges two locations by tearing a hole through the Nothing. | "It bridges two distant locations by tearing a hole through the prison dimension known as the Nothing, and when the resulting portal closes, an ancient, …" | W:Gate of the Feral Gods | VERIFIED (cite B4 ch15) | 2026-10-03 |

Limits: +25% to all stats per death; a new random weakness each death (often insta-death); every 5th death resets to L1, memory loss, must be re-bonded and renamed; no regen if it dies before level 5 or if the body is destroyed; full size doubles after 10 deaths. | "This particular pet is unique in that it will regenerate upon death as long as their body has not been destroyed." | W:Tummy Acher; W:Garret | VERIFIED (cite B7 ch5) | 2026-10-03 |

Limits: Race choice (Floor 3) forces two brothers into one body; the "life" ends the crawl for them; reset to Level X. | "Their game guide advised Dmitri to select the nodling race that would give him an extra life if he'd died." | W:Popov Brothers (Maxim _ Dmitri); W:Dmitri Popov; YT:TKcMxF2dA3w | VERIFIED (cite B5 ch41; 2 sources) | 2026-10-03 |

## 3. Loot box facts

| ID | Fact (paraphrase) | Source quote (<=25 words) | Source | Confidence | Date |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| L-001 | Six box tiers: Bronze < Silver < Gold < Platinum < Legendary < Celestial. | "Higher-tier boxes are far more elaborate; the Legendary Pet Box Carl opened during the tutorial featured intricately carved silver-and-gold patterns and spinning clockwork gears that …" | W:Loot Boxes | VERIFIED (cite B1 ch5) | 2026-10-03 |
| L-002 | Boxes sit locked in the inventory until the crawler is in a safe room; opened all at once in tier order. | "… boxes through the "Awards and Boxes" tab of their inventory, where the boxes stay locked until the crawler reaches a designated Safe Room or Guildhall." | W:Loot Boxes | VERIFIED (cite B1 ch2) | 2026-10-03 |
| L-003 | Box contents are 'semi-random' (random or hand-picked by the AI). | "The loot itself is "semi-random": a box's contents may be genuinely randomized, or the System AI may have hand-picked them to help or hurt a …" | W:Loot Boxes | VERIFIED (cite B1 ch29) | 2026-10-03 |
| L-004 | Benefactor (sponsor) boxes outclass ordinary boxes of the same tier and can hold off-world items. | "… same six quality tiers as every other box, but rank for rank they outclass ordinary loot: a bronze benefactor box beats a gold adventurer box." | W:Benefactor Box | VERIFIED (cite B1 ch4) | 2026-10-03 |
| L-005 | Boss Box tier scales with the boss tier. | "The box's tier, and the quality of the loot inside, scales with the difficulty of the boss defeated." | W:Boss Box | VERIFIED (cite B1 ch12) | 2026-10-03 |
| L-006 | Fan Boxes' contents are chosen by audience vote. | "Its contents are chosen by an audience vote, and the box typically cannot be opened for thirty hours after it is awarded." | W:Fan Box | VERIFIED (cite B2 ch13) | 2026-10-03 |
| L-007 | Only 2,145 Celestial boxes have ever been awarded. | "As a result, only 2,145 Celestial Boxes of any kind have been awarded in the history of Dungeon Crawler World." | W:Celestial | VERIFIED (page cite B2 epi) | 2026-10-03 |
| L-008 | No crawler has received more than four Celestial boxes; record season total is 18. | "No single crawler has ever received more than four Celestial Boxes, and no more than (18) have been distributed during a single season." | W:Celestial | VERIFIED (cite B2 epi) | 2026-10-03 |
| L-009 | Borant vetoed 82 Celestial boxes at the end of Floor 3 (Bandit / Fools Who Broke the Glass incident). | "The Borant Corporation successfully vetoed the award of 82 celestial boxes." | W:Celestial | VERIFIED (page cite B2 epi) | 2026-10-03 |
| L-010 | The Cuck Aquaman box is a Platinum compensation box of coupons (free Level-3 Personal Space, crafting tables, upgrades). | "Coupon for a free Personal Space, upgraded to level three." | W:It's Not My Fault You Fish-Headed Assholes Don't Properly Program Your Quests Box | VERIFIED (page cite B3 ch2) | 2026-10-03 |

| L-013 | Hunter-Killer Box pays a base of gold plus extra gold per hunter kill. | "Contains a thousand gold plus another thousand for each hunter kill gold coins" | W:Hunter-Killer Box | WIKI-ONLY | 2026-10-03 |

### 9.1 Floor 1 creatures, hazards and set-pieces (Pass 18, 2026-10-04; prefix M- continues mechanics rulings; feeds tables/canon-floor-1.json)
Source: local Fandom mirror pages (and one FW2 bestiary). Same caveat as section 0: nothing here was read against the novels. Quotes are wiki wording, not novel text. "page cite" = a chapter cite sits elsewhere in the same paragraph of the wiki page. Two rows (Thorn Cadaver, Hobgoblin maze) rest on one fan site and stay UNVERIFIED.

| ID | Ruling (paraphrase) | Source quote (<=25 words) | Source | Confidence | Date |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| M-263 | Floor 1 is a square grid of halls; each square holds four neighborhoods, each with its own local mob and neighborhood boss. | "Each square contains four neighborhoods, and each neighborhood has its own area-specific mob and neighborhood boss." | W:First Floor | VERIFIED (cite B1 ch8,15) | 2026-10-04 |
| M-264 | Floor 1 mobs run low level, and a neighborhood stops spawning its mobs once its boss is dead. | "stop spawning in a neighborhood after the local boss is killed." | W:First Floor | VERIFIED (cite B1 ch15) | 2026-10-04 |
| M-265 | Rats turn up in every neighborhood and act as the floor's janitor mobs, eating dead crawlers. | "These rats also function as the floor's "janitor mobs," consuming the corpses of fallen crawlers." | W:First Floor | VERIFIED (cite B1 ch8,30) | 2026-10-04 |
| M-266 | Rat bites and scratches can poison; rats burst messily when struck; they are small, hissing and numerous. | "Their bite and scratch attacks may inflict the Poison Debuff" | W:Rat-kin | VERIFIED (cite B1 ch8) | 2026-10-04 |
| M-267 | Basic Scatterers are bread-loaf-sized cockroach mobs that die easily; they nest in the Hoarder's neighborhood. | "Approximately the size and shape of a loaf of bread, basic scatterers are easily killed." | W:Scatterer | VERIFIED (cite B1 ch8) | 2026-10-04 |
| M-268 | Scatterer Brood Guardians are the tougher, cat-sized, fast-charging variant. | "Brood Guardians are approximately cat-sized, and quickly charge at their targets." | W:Scatterer | VERIFIED (page cite B1 ch10) | 2026-10-04 |
| M-269 | The Hoarder fights by retching Scatterer minions out of her own mouth. | "defends it by disgorging Scatterer minions from her own mouth." | W:Hoarder | VERIFIED (cite B1 ch11) | 2026-10-04 |
| M-270 | Early boss rooms seal behind the crawlers: the broken door re-forms and silver bars lock it shut one at a time. | "the shattered door reforms behind the crawlers, three glowing silver bars lock it in place one by one" | W:Hoarder | VERIFIED (cite B1 ch11) | 2026-10-04 |

### 9.2 Floor 2 rules, creatures, hazards, NPCs and set-pieces (Pass 19, 2026-10-04; prefix M- continues mechanics rulings; feeds tables/canon-floor-2.json, status "reserve")
Source: local Fandom mirror pages, one FW2 bestiary and one YouTube recap transcript (auto-captions), plus web chapter-summary snippets saved in `sources/web/f02-web-snippets.txt` (web-only rows have no quote and are labelled UNVERIFIED). Same caveat as section 0: nothing here was read against the novels. Quotes are wiki wording, not novel text. "page cite" = a chapter cite sits elsewhere in the same paragraph of the wiki page; "chapter-summary row" = the row comes from the wiki's Book 1 chapter table. Levels, difficulty, credits and loot amounts are deliberately not captured (Syndicate rules/10 and the loot decks own them). Existing Floor 2 anchors stay where they are: M-010..M-016 (safe rooms, stairwells), A-123/A-034/A-047/A-071/A-083/A-091/A-131/A-136 (achievements), B-004/B-005/B-029, H-004/H-021/H-028, T-007..T-009.

PREFERRED: Tusklings belong to Floor 1; the Floor 2 bestiary table row is a placement glitch. Not used in the Floor 2 canon table. | 2026-10-04 |

### 9.3 Premise verification: Transformation, dungeon shape, stairwells, progression and collapse fate (Pass 20, 2026-10-04; prefix M- continues mechanics rulings; feeds drafts/premise-verification.md)
Source: local Fandom mirror pages, one YouTube recap transcript set, fan-overview files (FW2) and web search snippets saved in `sources/web/premise-web-snippets.txt` (web-only rows have no quote and are labelled UNVERIFIED). Same caveat as section 0: nothing here was read against the novels. Quotes are wiki wording, not novel text. "[Claim n]" in a ruling refers to the five claims in `drafts/premise-verification.md`. "page cite" = a chapter cite sits elsewhere in the same paragraph of the wiki page. The per-floor stairwell rows come from the wiki's Floor Breakdown table, which has no quotable sentence, so they carry no quote; each was checked against the announced halving rule (150,000, halving and rounding up). Crawler counts, timers in days and stairwell counts are canon facts, not difficulty values; levels, credits and loot amounts are deliberately not captured. Existing anchors: T-001, T-002, R-024, R-042, M-016, M-039, M-277, M-335, M-366.

#### 9.3c Premise conflicts with the overview (X-066..X-075)

| ID | Topic | Reading A (quote or note) | Source A | Reading B (quote or note) | Source B | Status | Date |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| X-066 | Transformation: instant death vs "stasis" | overview says the aliens collapsed every interior structure, killed every human and put them in stasis | Caleb's overview (job brief 2026-10-04) | "anyone who was indoors when it happened died instantly" | W:Transformation | RESOLVED for canon. PREFERRED: instant death for anyone inside or under a roofed structure; no stasis for the dead appears in the pack. Stasis in canon is only the early-descent rule (M-277, tr_nostasis row). Keep "stasis" only as a house/inspired idea and label it so in the Core Book. | 2026-10-04 |
| X-067 | Transformation: "every human" vs only those indoors | overview says the aliens killed every human (then separately that people outside survived) | Caleb's overview (job brief 2026-10-04) | "Any living being inside or underneath such a structure was killed instantly." | W:Transformation | RESOLVED for canon. PREFERRED: only those inside or under a roof died; survivors were outdoors and the Collected are the dead (Book 4 spoiler). The overview's own two statements disagree unless read as "every human indoors". | 2026-10-04 |
| X-068 | Dungeon shape: "inverted pyramid" vs "inverted triangle" | overview says giant inverted pyramid | Caleb's overview (job brief 2026-10-04) | "the subterranean Dungeon looks like an inverted triangle" | W:Dungeon | OPEN (wording only). PREFERRED: say "inverted triangle" (seen from the side) for canon; "pyramid" is not used by any local source or cited web page, so tag it inspired. | 2026-10-04 |
| X-069 | Prize: "returns Earth" vs "win regency of the Earth" | overview says beating floor 18 wins and returns Earth | Caleb's overview (job brief 2026-10-04) | "Upon successful completion of level 18 of the World Dungeon, regency of this planet will revert to the successor." | W:Transformation | OPEN. PREFERRED: say the winner wins regency of Earth (the right to reclaim its matter, as presented to survivors). No source says Earth, the dead or the mined matter are restored; "returns Earth" is unverified. Note the announcement says "the successor", wording left unexplained on the wiki. | 2026-10-04 |
| X-070 | Win status: "nobody has beaten it" | overview says nobody has beaten it | Caleb's overview (job brief 2026-10-04) | "only one participant in the show's history has made it as far as the Thirteenth Floor" | W:Dungeon | RESOLVED (with a spoiler caveat). PREFERRED: no one in the show's history has finished (best prior result: one Crawler reached Floor 13). Book 8 spoilers on the Floor 18 page say the final door was opened for the Earth crawl, so the claim is only safe for the opening premise, not the whole series. | 2026-10-04 |
| X-071 | Start counts: 13 million vs 10 million vs "previous two million" | "Just under 13 million humans made it through the gates as crawlers" | W:Transformation | "roughly 1.3 million of the previous two million" | W:Second Floor | OPEN. PREFERRED: say "about 13 million entered, under 10 million by the first-floor seal, about 1.3 million at the start of Floor 2". Nerdist says ten million entered (WEB:nerdist-overview) and the Second Floor page's "previous two million" fits no other figure (the recap shows 3.5-4 million mid-floor). Counts stay unverified against the novel. | 2026-10-04 |
| X-072 | Floor 4 survivors: about 1,400 vs 178,887 | "Approximately 1,400 crawlers escaped Floor 4 alive." | FW2:book3-overview | "Remaining Crawlers \| 178,887" | FW2:book4-overview | OPEN. PREFERRED: use the 178,887 figure for the start of Floor 5 (matches the tens-of-thousands collapse toll). The 1,400 may be a subset or a slip in one fan overview; unverified. | 2026-10-04 |
| X-073 | Guarded stairwells: Third Floor vs Fourth Floor | "Beginning on the Third Floor, stairwells must be unlocked by completing quests" | W:Dungeon | "from the Fourth Floor onward they stop populating randomly and become guarded" | W:Stairwells | OPEN. Both are wiki statements; the Stairwells table leaves Floor 3 blank. PREFERRED for the Core Book: say "from about the Third or Fourth Floor stairwells are guarded or unlocked", not an exact floor. | 2026-10-04 |
| X-074 | Leaving the Dungeon: "may not leave" vs Exit Deals | "If you enter, you may not leave until you have either completed all 18 levels of the World Dungeon" | W:Transformation | "Although crawlers can accept Exit Deals after entering the Tenth Floor" | W:Exit Deal | RESOLVED. PREFERRED: the announcement's "or if you meet certain other requirements" is the Exit Deal route (indenture to the production company, from Floor 10). Do not tell players winning is the only way out. | 2026-10-04 |
| X-075 | NPC fate at floor collapse: sleep vs not surviving | "go to sleep until the next Dungeon opens" | W:Lorelai | "It occupies a separate pocket dimension where NPCs and mobs can survive the collapse of a dungeon floor" | YT:kk6nspvVf_M | OPEN. Canon never states the NPC/AI fate at collapse in one authoritative line. Mob belief (Rory, Lorelai; wiki page uncited) is sleep until the next Dungeon; the Pineapple Cabaret recap implies NPCs and mobs normally do not survive; Stairwells says everything left behind dies. PREFERRED: tag unverified; for the RPG treat Crawlers left behind as dead (canon) and NPCs as reset or recycled (inspired). | 2026-10-04 |

### 9.4 Floor 3 (The Over City): setting, laws, timer, safe rooms, stairwells, night mobs and Elites (Pass 21, 2026-10-05; prefix M- continues mechanics rulings)
Source: local Fandom mirror pages (Third Floor, Village Guard Swordsman, Gate Pass, Street Urchin, Shadow Leaks, Elite), the Book 2 recap transcript (YT:sS36rv-jPLg) and one fan overview (FW2). Same caveat as section 0: nothing here was read against the novels. Quotes are wiki or caption wording, not novel text. "page cite" = a chapter cite sits elsewhere in the same paragraph. Race/class selection on Floor 3 is already in sections 11-12; Floor 3 achievements are in section 1. Levels, credits and loot amounts are deliberately not captured. Existing anchors: M-277, M-629, R-042, R-043, X-031, X-062, L-009.

| M-652 | It is the first of six urban Volcano floors (3, 6, 9, 12, 15, 18 per the page's speculation section), layers of one giant crater. | "The Over City is the first of six special urban "Volcano" storyline floors" | W:Third Floor | VERIFIED (cite B2 ch3) | 2026-10-05 |

### 9.7 Clubs (Desperado Club, Club Vanquisher) and Personal Spaces / Bopca pricing (Pass 33, 2026-10-05; prefix M- continues; feeds catalog/clubs-and-personal-spaces-canon.md)

Floor 3 opens the clubs and Floor 4 opens Personal Spaces. Rows are rules a GM can run at the table (access, safety, prices, haggling, unlock floors). Gold prices are canon book prices, not RPG credit prices. Quotes from local Fandom mirrors plus one Book 3 recap transcript. Script work/pass33/rows.py (do not re-run).

### 9.8 Quest system, quest boxes and the Floor 3 quests (Pass 34, 2026-10-06; prefix M- continues; feeds catalog/quest-system-canon.md)

Quests start on Floor 3. Rows are rules a GM can run at the table: how a quest is announced, who receives it, what it pays, how it fails, and how the Floor 3 quests played out. Builds on M-020 (quests lapse off-floor), M-718 (no quests on Floors 1-2), M-769/M-776 (Desperado quest board) and M-819 (Quest Screen upgrade), plus achievements A-004, A-020, A-048, A-063, A-065, A-095. Gold amounts are canon book amounts, not RPG credit prices. All quotes are from the local Fandom mirror. TODO 55 named Vengeance of the Daughter and Where the Sidewalk Ends as Floor 3 quests; they are really Floor 6 and Floor 8 quests (Vengeance has its Floor 3 roots in the circus show), so they are logged here with their real floors. Script work/pass34/rows.py (do not re-run).

| ID | Topic | Fact (paraphrase) | Source quote (<=25 words) | Source | Confidence | Date |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| M-826 | Quest tab unlock | The Quests tab of the crawler interface first unlocks on Floor 3 (confirms M-718). | "tracked in a dedicated tab of the crawler interface that unlocks on the third floor" | W:Quest | VERIFIED (cite B2 ch4) | 2026-10-06 |
| M-827 | Quests pay XP and loot | Quests are a main source of experience and loot. | "They are a primary source of experience and loot." | W:Quest | VERIFIED (cite B2 ch7) | 2026-10-06 |
| M-828 | Quest announcement staging | A new quest lands with a trumpet chime, illusion streamers and title-card text in midair. | "announced with a trumpet chime, illusionary streamers, and text that slams into place in midair like a title card" | W:Quest | VERIFIED (cite B2 ch4) | 2026-10-06 |
| M-829 | Quest text shape | Every quest reads the same way: AI-voice flavor paragraph, a plain objective, then a Reward line. | "a flavor paragraph in the AI's voice, a plainly worded objective, and a Reward line naming the payout" | W:Quest | VERIFIED (cite B2 ch4) | 2026-10-06 |
| M-830 | Quest triggers | Most quests fire from the crawler's own actions or location, such as walking into a district with a running storyline. | "Most quests trigger on a crawler's own actions or location, such as entering a district where a storyline is already running." | W:Quest | VERIFIED (cite B2 ch4) | 2026-10-06 |
| M-831 | NPC-given quests | NPC quests come face to face, usually from desperate pub locals, and are usually low-value busywork. | "handed out face to face by NPCs, most often by desperate locals in pubs, and tend to be low-value busywork" | W:Quest | VERIFIED (cite B2 ch14) | 2026-10-06 |
| M-832 | Quest-giver star | Quest-givers sometimes show a star over their minimap dot, but not always. | "quest-givers sometimes carry a star over their minimap icon, though not every quest is flagged that way" | W:Quest | VERIFIED (cite B2 ch14) | 2026-10-06 |
| M-833 | Quest map markers | Objectives, targets and destinations are often marked on the map. | "Objectives, targets, and destinations are frequently marked on the crawler's map." | W:Quest | VERIFIED (cite B2 ch24) | 2026-10-06 |
| M-834 | Quest Update | A Quest Update can rewrite an active quest in place, redirecting the goal and resetting its clock. | "A Quest Update can revise an active quest in place, redirecting the original objective and resetting its clock." | W:Quest | VERIFIED (cite B2 ch25) | 2026-10-06 |
| M-835 | Event quest saferoom lockout | In a floor-wide event quest crawlers get a short window to reach a saferoom; then saferooms in the zone lock until it ends. | "crawlers are given a brief window to reach a saferoom, after which saferoom access inside the quest zone is shut off until the event concludes" | W:Quest | VERIFIED (cite B2 ch24) | 2026-10-06 |
| M-836 | Quest Chat | Event quests open a temporary Quest Chat listing every participant. | "a temporary Quest Chat channel opens listing every participant" | W:Quest | VERIFIED (cite B2 ch24) | 2026-10-06 |
| M-837 | Johnny Quest map perk | Completing a first quest alive (A-065) makes new quest locations sometimes appear on the map. | "new quest locations will sometimes appear on your map" | W:Quest | VERIFIED (cite B2 ch13) | 2026-10-06 |
| M-838 | Pass or fail quests | A pass-or-fail quest states its failure condition up front and stays live only while its trigger holds (e.g. staying aboard a train). | "A pass or fail quest names its failure condition in the quest text and stays active only while its trigger condition holds" | W:Quest | VERIFIED (cite B3 ch12) | 2026-10-06 |
| M-839 | Solving before issue | Solving a quest before the AI issues it gives Premature Questjaculation and forfeits the box (adds a chapter cite to WIKI-ONLY row A-095). | "A quest can be resolved before it is ever issued, which earns the Premature Questjaculation achievement and forfeits the box" | W:Quest | VERIFIED (cite B5 ch28) | 2026-10-06 |
| M-840 | Option-menu quests | Some quests offer a numbered menu of outcomes, each with its own reward line. | "Some quests are presented as a numbered menu of options, each with its own reward line" | W:Quest | VERIFIED (cite B6 ch62) | 2026-10-06 |
| M-841 | Individual quests | Individual quests go to one crawler only. | "Individual Quests are issued to a single crawler rather than to a party or a wider body of crawlers." | W:Quest | VERIFIED (page cite B4 ch32) | 2026-10-06 |
| M-842 | Individual via god | A quest can be individual because it comes from a god only that crawler worships. | "A quest may be individual because it comes from a god only that crawler worships" | W:Quest | VERIFIED (cite B4 ch32) | 2026-10-06 |
| M-843 | Escort quests | Escort quests ask for safe delivery of a creature, not a kill. | "Escort quests are a recognized variant, tasking a crawler with delivering a creature safely to a destination rather than killing anything." | W:Quest | VERIFIED (cite B6 ch26) | 2026-10-06 |
| M-844 | Party quests | Party quests go to every party member at once. | "Party Quests are issued to every member of a party at once." | W:Quest | VERIFIED (page cite B2 ch4) | 2026-10-06 |

| M-846 | Group quests: host party | Group quests name a host party that cannot opt out and open a shared quest chat. | "They designate a host party that is explicitly barred from opting out, and open a shared quest chat listing every participant" | W:Quest | VERIFIED (cite B2 ch24) | 2026-10-06 |
| M-847 | Regional quests | Regional quests cover every crawler in a set area of the floor, between group and world scale, with a host squad that cannot withdraw. | "covering every crawler inside a defined geographic area of the floor rather than a blast radius or the entire level" | W:Quest | VERIFIED (cite B6 ch62) | 2026-10-06 |

| X-086 | Floor-bound quests vs multi-floor quests | "Most quests are optional and bound to the floor that issued them" | W:Quest | "a standing order to kill Hellik before the twelfth floor" | W:Quest | RESOLVED as an exception, not a contradiction: ordinary quests lapse off-floor (M-020), but deity quests (Emberus, cite B4 ch32) and show-driven quest lines (Vengeance of the Daughter set up on F3, issued on F6, M-892) can span floors. For the RPG: a quest only carries over if its text names a later deadline. | 2026-10-06 |

### 9.9 Floor 2 refinement, second pass (Pass 35, 2026-10-06; prefix M- continues mechanics rulings; feeds tables/canon-floor-2.json, status "reserve")
Source: local Fandom mirror pages (Second Floor, Rage Elemental, Mother of All Bombs (MOAB), Meadow Lark, Meadow Lark Elder Care Facility, Imani, Brandon Andrews, Ralph); their live revisions were checked on 2026-10-06 and none changed after the mirror was taken (Donut/Floor 2 still has a newer edit, not mined). New generated fan wiki pages saved under sources/fanwiki/ (book1-locations-dungeon-floor-2, book1-events-rage-elemental-encounter, book1-characters-brandon-and-crew, book1-characters-mongo, plus Li Jun, Zhang and System AI pages not yet mined), and the Book 1 recap transcript (YT:j6jRXuCgsAA). Same caveat as section 0: nothing here was read against the novels. Levels, difficulty, credits and loot amounts left out.

| ID | Ruling (paraphrase) | Source quote (<=25 words) | Source | Confidence | Date |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| M-900 | The Rage Elemental has two known attacks: its claws and a limited-range Reverse Gravity Spell (second family for M-465). | "Had two known attacks: powerful claws and a reverse-gravity spell with limited range." | FW2:book1-events-rage-elemental-encounter; W:Rage Elemental | VERIFIED (2 sources: FW2 + Fandom, cite B1 ch38,40) | 2026-10-06 |
| M-901 | A fan wiki gives the ejected elemental 90 to 120 seconds to come back, against the Fandom 75 to 120 seconds (M-468); see X-087 (rb-10). | "it would return within 90–120 seconds" | FW2:book1-events-rage-elemental-encounter | UNVERIFIED (conflicts with M-468; X-087) | 2026-10-06 |

Recap of Book 1 says a Sapper's Table 'he can get in a safe room store'; Sapper's Table infobox lists Marketplace. | B1 ch9,30,41 | "which Safe Rooms do not sell until the Fourth Floor." | W:Crafting | UNVERIFIED (unresolved (wiki vs yt)) | "a sapper's table, which he can get in a safe room store" | YT:j6jRXuCgsAA | 2026-10-03 |

## 11. Races and Floor 3 race selection (added 2026-10-03, Pass 7; prefix R- continues 4e, conflicts X-031..X-041 in section 8)
Strictness note for this section: VERIFIED means (a) the quoted sentence carries a wiki chapter cite (`cite B# ch#`; `page cite` = the cite is elsewhere on the page's selection text), and/or (b) a second source family (YouTube recap `YT`, NL explainer `NL`) says the same thing; the wiki cites have not been checked against the novels. Where a page says a race is crawler-choosable but names no crawler and gives no cite, the row is UNVERIFIED or WIKI-ONLY. Race rows extend (do not replace) the 4e rows R-001..R-024 (for example R-003 Cat, R-004 Caprid, R-009 Doppelganger, R-011 Hobgoblin, R-014 Changeling).
Section counts: 57 rows (R-025..R-081): 48 VERIFIED, 8 WIKI-ONLY, 1 UNVERIFIED; plus 11 conflict rows X-031..X-041 in section 8. Detail catalog: `catalog/race-selection-canon.md`.
Columns: ID | Subject | Key facts (own words) | Named crawler(s) | Cite | Quote 1 (<=25 words) | Source 1 | Confidence | Quote 2 | Source 2 | Date

## 12. Classes and Floor 3/6/9 class selection (added 2026-10-04, Pass 8; prefix C- continues 4d, conflicts X-042..X-047 in section 8)
Strictness note for this section: VERIFIED means (a) the quoted sentence carries a wiki chapter cite (`cite B# ch#`; `page cite` = the cite is elsewhere on the page), and/or (b) a second source family (YouTube recap `YT`, NL explainer `NL`) says the same thing; the wiki cites have not been checked against the novels. Class rows extend (do not replace) the 4d rows C-001..C-013. Detail catalog: `catalog/class-selection-canon.md`.
Section counts: 37 rows (C-014..C-050): process C-014..C-032 and examples C-033..C-050; plus 6 conflict rows X-042..X-047 in section 8.
Columns: ID | Subject | Key facts (own words) | Named crawler(s) | Cite | Quote 1 (<=25 words) | Source 1 | Confidence | Quote 2 | Source 2 | Date

## 17. Upgrade Patches and patch garments (added 2026-10-04, Pass 16; prefix M- continues mechanics for process rules, I- for per-patch rows, conflicts X-057..X-058)
Feeds `catalog/upgrade-patches-canon.md`. Sources: `raw/pages/` (Upgrade Patch, Enchanted Anarchist's Battle Rattle, the nine patch pages, Soul Reaper Skill, Soul Poisoning, Mysterious Bone Key Benefit, Book Burner Spell, Climb Benefit), `sources/fanwiki/book4-equipment-enchanted-anarchists-battle-rattle.txt`, and YouTube recaps `7Q49XeziMX0` (B6), `FurNY4o0uCo` (B7), `TKcMxF2dA3w` (B5). Scavenger's Daughter basics already live in I-005 and M-084; Fashion of the Gods! is A-041. Not checked against the novels' text.

### 17.1 Process rules

| ID | Ruling (paraphrase) | Source quote (<=25 words) | Source | Confidence | Date |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| M-240 | Patches are "fleeting": you can take one off, but removing it destroys it. The System repeats this warning on every patch. | "Warning: Upgrade patches are fleeting items. You may remove them, but they will be destroyed in the process." | W:Earth Upgrade Patch | VERIFIED (page cite B4 ch28; same line on every patch page) | 2026-10-04 |
| M-241 | A patch only works when affixed to an "eligible garment"; the Battle Rattle jacket is the canon example and holds as many patches as physically fit. | "its ability to host as many upgrade patches as you can fit makes this one of the best, most upgradeable protection items in the game" | W:Enchanted Anarchist's Battle Rattle; FW2:book4-equipment-enchanted-anarchists-battle-rattle | VERIFIED (2 families; FW2 cites B4 ch28) | 2026-10-04 |
| M-242 | Garment scaling: the plain jacket gives +1 to all base stats, plus another +1 to all base stats for each compatible patch sewn on. | "This base stat upgrade increases by +1 for every compatible patch that is added to this jacket." | W:Enchanted Anarchist's Battle Rattle; FW2:book4-equipment-enchanted-anarchists-battle-rattle | VERIFIED (2 families) | 2026-10-04 |
| M-243 | An eligible Back Patch doubles that per-patch stat bonus. | "If an eligible Back Patch is added, this benefit is doubled." | W:Enchanted Anarchist's Battle Rattle; FW2:book4-equipment-enchanted-anarchists-battle-rattle | VERIFIED (2 families) | 2026-10-04 |
| M-244 | Only one back patch may be affixed at a time. | "You may only affix one back patch at a time." | W:Scavenger's Daughter Upgrade Patch | VERIFIED (page cite B6 ch3) | 2026-10-04 |
| M-245 | Patch sizes seen in canon: Small, Medium, and Back Patch (full size). | "As a back patch, the Scavenger's Daughter is full sized, and only one back patch may be affixed at a time." | W:Scavenger's Daughter Upgrade Patch | VERIFIED (page cite B6 ch3; Small/Medium from the type field of each patch page) | 2026-10-04 |
| M-246 | Craftsmanship matters: a finely made patch (Reaper Spider silk, hand-stitched) has enhanced upgrades; screen-printed ones are the cheap tier. | "This patch is finely made, which means all below upgrades are enhanced." | W:Bomb Upgrade Patch | VERIFIED (page cite B5 ch36) | 2026-10-04 |
| M-247 | Order trick: Carl sewed the small peace patch on first so the back patch's doubling would cover all his regular patches. | "He sewed a small peace symbol patch onto the Enchanted Anarchist's Battle Rattle first so the doubling effect would catch all of his regular patches" | W:Scavenger's Daughter Upgrade Patch | VERIFIED (page cite B6 ch3) | 2026-10-04 |
| M-248 | A divine patch hides its upgrades until it is both affixed and worn. | "(Hidden until it is affixed and then worn.)" | W:Scavenger's Daughter Upgrade Patch | VERIFIED (page cite B6 ch3) | 2026-10-04 |
| M-249 | Divine patch: buffs (and possibly debuffs) change with proximity to particular deities and demons. | "it gains differing abilities, buffs, and possibly debuffs depending on your proximity to certain deities and demons." | W:Scavenger's Daughter Upgrade Patch | VERIFIED (page cite B6 ch3) | 2026-10-04 |
| M-250 | When the deity leaves, its temporary effect fades over ten minutes. | "When the deity departs, the benefit fades over the following ten minutes." | W:Scavenger's Daughter Upgrade Patch | VERIFIED (page cite B6 ch16) | 2026-10-04 |
| M-251 | Effects from several deities present at once stack. | "Effects from multiple deities stack," | W:Scavenger's Daughter Upgrade Patch | VERIFIED (page cite B6 ch17) | 2026-10-04 |
| M-252 | Range limit: a deity who is in the realm but far away is reported as too distant to trigger the patch. | "A deity who enters the realm but remains far away returns a notice that they are too distant to activate the patch." | W:Scavenger's Daughter Upgrade Patch | VERIFIED (page cite B7 ch59) | 2026-10-04 |
| M-253 | Soul Reaper capacity grows every time the essence bar is filled and then emptied (each strike stronger, needs more kills). | "The total amount of soul essence you may store increases each time your essence bar is topped off and then drained." | W:Soul Reaper Skill | VERIFIED (page cite B6 ch3; extends M-084) | 2026-10-04 |
| M-254 | Purity bonus: a bar filled from a single species hits about twice as hard, with stronger side effects. | "Essence drawn entirely from a single species produces a strike roughly twice as powerful, with stronger residual effects" | W:Scavenger's Daughter Upgrade Patch | VERIFIED (page cite B6 ch27) | 2026-10-04 |
| M-255 | Carrying a full soul bar down the stairs causes Soul Poisoning (worsening pain); taking off the jacket while full is dangerous. | "Oh, and don’t try unequipping that jacket while your soul power is full." | W:Soul Poisoning | VERIFIED (page cite B7 ch1; YT FurNY4o0uCo agrees on the cause; extends M-084) | 2026-10-04 |
